\begin{array}[]{l}{\tt m}(t)\;=\;{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}\!\left<s_{t}\right>)\;=\;{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{t}\right>\,s)\;=\;{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{t}\right>)\uplus{\tt m}(s)\\
\qquad\;\succ\;{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{u}\right>)\uplus{\tt m}(s)\;=\;{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{u}\right>\,s)\;=\;{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}\!\left<s_{u}\right>)\;=\;{\tt m}(u)\end{array}

\mathtt{F}=s\,\mathtt{F}^{\prime}
: By
i.h.
on

\mathtt{F}^{\prime}
, one has that

{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{t}\right>)\succ{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{u}\right>)
thus:

\begin{array}[]{l}{\tt m}(t)\;=\;{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}\!\left<s_{t}\right>)\;=\;{\tt m}(s\,\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{t}\right>)\;=\;{\tt m}(s)\uplus{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{t}\right>)\\
\qquad\;\succ\;{\tt m}(s)\uplus{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{u}\right>)\;=\;{\tt m}(s\,\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{u}\right>)\;=\;{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}\!\left<s_{u}\right>)\;=\;{\tt m}(u)\end{array}

\mathtt{F}=\mathtt{F}^{\prime}[y\backslash s]
: By
i.h.
on

\mathtt{F}^{\prime}
, one has that

{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{t}\right>)\succ{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{u}\right>)
. Moreover, by

Lemma

41
, one deduces
that
{\tt P}_{\!y}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{t}\right>)\geq{\tt P}_{\!y}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{u}\right>)
, thus:

\begin{array}[]{l}{\tt m}(t)\;=\;{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}\!\left<s_{t}\right>)\;=\;{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{t}\right>[y\backslash s])\;=\;{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{t}\right>)\uplus\max(1,{\tt P}_{\!y}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{t}\right>))\cdot{\tt m}(s)\\
\qquad\;\succ\;{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{u}\right>)\uplus\max(1,{\tt P}_{\!y}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{u}\right>))\cdot{\tt m}(s)\;=\;{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{u}\right>[y\backslash s])\;=\;{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}\!\left<s_{u}\right>)\;=\;{\tt m}(u)\end{array}

\mathtt{F}=s[y\backslash\mathtt{F}^{\prime}]
: By
i.h.
on

\mathtt{F}^{\prime}
, one has that

{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{t}\right>)\succ{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{u}\right>)
thus:

\begin{array}[]{l}{\tt m}(t)\;=\;{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}\!\left<s_{t}\right>)\;=\;{\tt m}(s[y\backslash\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{t}\right>])\;=\;{\tt m}(s)\uplus\max(1,{\tt P}_{\!y}(s))\cdot{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{t}\right>)\\
\qquad\;\succ\;{\tt m}(s)\uplus\max(1,{\tt P}_{\!y}(s))\cdot{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{u}\right>)\;=\;{\tt m}(s[y\backslash\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{u}\right>])\;=\;{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}\!\left<s_{u}\right>)\;=\;{\tt m}(u)\end{array}

\mathtt{F}={\tt der}\!\left(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\right)
: By
i.h.
on

\mathtt{F}^{\prime}
, one has that

{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{t}\right>)\succ{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{u}\right>)
thus:

{\tt m}(t)={\tt m}(\mathtt{F}\!\left<s_{t}\right>)={\tt m}({\tt der}\!\left(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{t}\right>\right))={\tt m}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{t}\right>)\succ{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{u}\right>)={\tt m}({\tt der}\!\left(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{u}\right>\right))={\tt m}(\mathtt{F}\!\left<s_{u}\right>)={\tt m}(u)

\mathtt{F}=\oc\mathtt{F}^{\prime}
: By
i.h.
on

\mathtt{F}^{\prime}
, one has that

{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{t}\right>)\succ{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{u}\right>)
thus:

{\tt m}(t)={\tt m}(\mathtt{F}\!\left<s_{t}\right>)={\tt m}(\oc\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{t}\right>)={\tt m}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{t}\right>)\succ{\tt m}(\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{u}\right>)={\tt m}(\oc\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<s_{u}\right>)={\tt m}(\mathtt{F}\!\left<s_{u}\right>)={\tt m}(u)

∎

Corollary 43
.

The reduction
\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}
is terminating.

Proof.

Let
t\in\Lambda_{\oc}
. As the finite multisets over natural
numbers is well-ordered by
\prec
, we can proceed by induction on

{\tt m}(t)
. If
t
is a
{\tt s}\oc
-normal form, then it is
trivially terminating. Otherwise,
t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}u
,
then
{\tt m}(t)\succ{\tt m}(u)

(
Lemma

42
), and so
u
is
strongly
{\tt s}\oc
-normalizing, by the
i.h.
Therefore,
t
is
strongly
{\tt s}\oc
-normalizing.
∎

A.2.3
Confluence of
\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}

Corollary 44
.

The reduction
\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}
is confluent.

Proof.

By
Lemmas

35
,
35
and
43
, using Newman’s Lemma.
∎

A.3
Confluence of
\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}}

Lemma 45
.

The reductions
\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}\cup\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt d}\oc\right>}
and
\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}

strongly commute.

Proof.

Let
t,u_{1},u_{2}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that
t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}u_{1}
and
t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}u_{2}
for some
\mathcal{R}\in\{{\tt dB},{\tt d}\oc\}
. Let us show
that there exists
s\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that
u_{1}\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}s
and
u_{2}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}s
.

By induction on
t
:

t=x
: Impossible since it contradicts the hypothesis

t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}u_{2}
.

t=\lambda x.t^{\prime}
: Since by hypothesis
t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}u_{1}
and
t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}u_{2}
, then necessarily
u_{1}=\lambda x.u^{\prime}_{1}
and
u_{2}=\lambda x.u^{\prime}_{2}
for some
u^{\prime}_{1},u^{\prime}_{2}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that
t^{\prime}\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}u^{\prime}_{1}
and

t^{\prime}\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}u^{\prime}_{2}
. By
i.h.
on
t^{\prime}
, there
exists
s^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that
u^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}s^{\prime}
and
u^{\prime}_{2}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}s^{\prime}
. We set
s:=\lambda x.s^{\prime}
since
by contextual closure
u_{1}=\lambda x.u^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}\lambda x.s^{\prime}=s^{\prime}
and
u_{2}=\lambda x.u^{\prime}_{2}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}\lambda x.s^{\prime}=s^{\prime}
.
Graphically,

\begin{array}[]{lll}\lambda x.t^{\prime}&\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&\lambda x.u^{\prime}_{1}\\[5.69046pt]
\;\;\downarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}&&\;\;\downarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}\\[5.69046pt]
\lambda x.u^{\prime}_{2}&\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&\lambda x.s^{\prime}\end{array}

t=t_{1}t_{2}
: We distinguish two cases:

t
is the
{\tt dB}
-redex reduced in the step

t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}u_{1}
: Then
t_{1}=\mathtt{L}\!\left<\lambda x.t^{\prime}_{1}\right>
and
u_{1}=\mathtt{L}\!\left<t^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>
. By hypothesis

\mathtt{L}\!\left<\lambda x.t^{\prime}_{1}\right>t_{2}\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}u_{2}
so that four different cases
can be distinguished:

\bullet

u_{2}=\mathtt{L}\!\left<\lambda x.u^{\prime}_{1}\right>t_{2}

for some
u^{\prime}_{1}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that
t^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}u^{\prime}_{1}
: We set
s:=\mathtt{L}\!\left<u^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>
which concludes this case
since by contextual closure
u_{1}=\mathtt{L}\!\left<t^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}\mathtt{L}\!\left<u^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>=s
and
u_{2}=\mathtt{L}\!\left<\lambda x.u^{\prime}_{1}\right>t_{2}\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}\mathtt{L}\!\left<u^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>=s
. Graphically,

\begin{array}[]{lll}\mathtt{L}\!\left<\lambda x.t^{\prime}_{1}\right>t_{2}&\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}&\mathtt{L}\!\left<t^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>\\[5.69046pt]
\;\;\downarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}&&\;\;\downarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}\\[5.69046pt]
\mathtt{L}\!\left<\lambda x.u^{\prime}_{1}\right>t_{2}&\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}&\mathtt{L}\!\left<u^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>\end{array}

\bullet

u_{2}=\mathtt{L}\!\left<\lambda x.t^{\prime}_{1}\right>u^{\prime}_{2}

for some
u^{\prime}_{2}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that
t_{2}\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}u^{\prime}_{2}
: We set
s:=\mathtt{L}\!\left<t^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash u^{\prime}_{2}]\right>
which concludes this case
since by contextual closure
u_{1}=\mathtt{L}\!\left<t^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}\mathtt{L}\!\left<t^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash u^{\prime}_{2}]\right>=s
and
u_{2}=\mathtt{L}\!\left<\lambda x.t^{\prime}_{1}\right>u^{\prime}_{2}\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}\mathtt{L}\!\left<t^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash u^{\prime}_{2}]\right>=s
. Graphically,

\begin{array}[]{lll}\mathtt{L}\!\left<\lambda x.t^{\prime}_{1}\right>t_{2}&\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}&\mathtt{L}\!\left<t^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>\\[5.69046pt]
\;\;\downarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}&&\;\;\downarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}\\[5.69046pt]
\mathtt{L}\!\left<\lambda x.t^{\prime}_{1}\right>u^{\prime}_{2}&\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}&\mathtt{L}\!\left<t^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash u^{\prime}_{2}]\right>\end{array}

\bullet

u_{2}=\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\!\left<\lambda x.t^{\prime}_{1}\right>t_{2}

for some context
\mathtt{L}^{\prime}
such that
\mathtt{L}\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}\mathtt{L}^{\prime}
: We set
s:=\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\!\left<t^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>
which concludes this case
since by

Lemma

29
,

u_{1}=\mathtt{L}\!\left<t^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\!\left<t^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>=s
and
u_{2}=\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\!\left<\lambda x.t^{\prime}_{1}\right>t_{2}\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\!\left<t^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>=s
. Graphically,

\begin{array}[]{lll}\mathtt{L}\!\left<\lambda x.t^{\prime}_{1}\right>t_{2}&\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}&\mathtt{L}\!\left<t^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>\\[5.69046pt]
\;\;\downarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}&&\;\;\downarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}\\[5.69046pt]
\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\!\left<\lambda x.t^{\prime}_{1}\right>t_{2}&\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}&\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\!\left<t^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>\end{array}

\bullet

\mathtt{L}=\mathtt{L}_{1}\!\left<\mathtt{L}_{3}[y\backslash\mathtt{L}_{2}\!\left<\oc t^{\prime\prime}_{1}\right>]\right>
,
u_{2}=\mathtt{L}_{1}\!\left<\mathtt{L}_{2}\!\left<\mathtt{L}_{3}\{y\backslash t^{\prime\prime}_{1}\}\!\left<\lambda x.t^{\prime}_{1}\{y\backslash t^{\prime\prime}_{1}\}\right>\right>\right>t_{2}

for some contexts
\mathtt{L}_{1},\mathtt{L}_{2},\mathtt{L}_{3}
and some term
t^{\prime\prime}_{1}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
: We
set
s:=\mathtt{L}_{1}\!\left<\mathtt{L}_{2}\!\left<\mathtt{L}_{3}\{y\backslash t^{\prime\prime}_{1}\}\!\left<t^{\prime}_{1}\{y\backslash t^{\prime\prime}_{1}\}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>\right>\right>

which concludes this case since by
\alpha
-conversion
y\notin{\tt fv}(t_{2})
thus
t_{2}\{x\backslash t^{\prime\prime}_{1}\}=t_{2}
and
therefore: Graphically,

\begin{array}[]{lll}\mathtt{L}_{1}\!\left<\mathtt{L}_{3}\!\left<\lambda x.t^{\prime}_{1}\right>[y\backslash\mathtt{L}_{2}\!\left<\oc t^{\prime\prime}_{1}\right>]\right>t_{2}&\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}&\mathtt{L}_{1}\!\left<\mathtt{L}_{3}\!\left<t^{\prime}_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]\right>[y\backslash\mathtt{L}_{2}\!\left<\oc t^{\prime\prime}_{1}\right>]\right>\\[5.69046pt]

t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}u_{2}
, then necessarily
u_{1}={\tt der}\!\left(u^{\prime}_{1}\right)
and
u_{2}={\tt der}\!\left(u^{\prime}_{2}\right)
for some
u^{\prime}_{1},u^{\prime}_{2}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that
t^{\prime}_{0}\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}u^{\prime}_{1}
and
t^{\prime}_{0}\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}u^{\prime}_{2}
. By
i.h.
on

t^{\prime}
, there exists
s^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that
u^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}s^{\prime}
and
u^{\prime}_{2}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}s^{\prime}
. We set
s:={\tt der}\!\left(s^{\prime}\right)
since
by contextual closure
u_{1}={\tt der}\!\left(u^{\prime}_{1}\right)\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}{\tt der}\!\left(s^{\prime}\right)=s^{\prime}
and
u_{2}={\tt der}\!\left(u^{\prime}_{2}\right)\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}{\tt der}\!\left(s^{\prime}\right)=s^{\prime}
.
Graphically,

\begin{array}[]{lll}{\tt der}\!\left(t^{\prime}_{0}\right)&\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&{\tt der}\!\left(u^{\prime}_{1}\right)\\[5.69046pt]
\;\;\downarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}&&\;\;\downarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}\\[5.69046pt]
{\tt der}\!\left(u^{\prime}_{1}\right)&\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&{\tt der}\!\left(s^{\prime}\right)\end{array}

t=\oc t^{\prime}
: Since by hypothesis
t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}u_{1}
and
t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}u_{2}
, then necessarily
u_{1}=\oc u^{\prime}_{1}
and
u_{2}=\oc u^{\prime}_{2}
for some
u^{\prime}_{1},u^{\prime}_{2}\in\Lambda_{\oc}

such that
t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}u^{\prime}_{1}
and
t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}u^{\prime}_{2}
. By
i.h.
on
t^{\prime}
, there exists

s^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that
u^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}s^{\prime}
and
u^{\prime}_{2}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}s^{\prime}
. We set
s:=\oc s^{\prime}
since by
contextual closure
u_{1}=\oc u^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}\oc s^{\prime}=s^{\prime}
and
u_{2}=\oc u^{\prime}_{2}\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}\oc s^{\prime}=s^{\prime}
. Graphically,

\begin{array}[]{lll}\oc t^{\prime}&\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&\oc u^{\prime}_{1}\\[5.69046pt]
\;\;\downarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}&&\;\;\downarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}\\[5.69046pt]
\oc u^{\prime}_{2}&\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<\mathcal{R}\right>}&\oc s^{\prime}\end{array}

∎

Corollary 46
.

The reductions
\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt dB}\right>}\cup\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt d}\oc\right>}
and
\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}\!\left<{\tt s}\oc\right>}

commute.

Proof.

Immediate consequence of

Lemma

45
.
∎

See
1

Proof.

(Surface)
Proof of confluence of the
surface reduction can be found in
[
20
]
.

(Full)
By

Corollaries

31
,
44
and
46
, using the Hindley–Rosen lemma

[
18
, Prop. 3.3.5]
.
∎

Appendix B
Proofs of
Section

3

Lemma 47
.

Let
\Pi\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma\vdash t:\sigma
. If

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma\right)
then there are
\mathtt{T}
and

\Pi^{\prime}
such that
\Pi^{\prime}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\emptyset\vdash\mathtt{T}\!\left<t\right>:\sigma
, in particular
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\sigma\right)
.

Proof.

Let
\Pi\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma\vdash t:\sigma
. We reason by induction
on the number of variables in
{\tt dom}(\Gamma)
:

\Gamma=\emptyset
: We set
\mathtt{T}\coloneqq\diamond
, so
\mathtt{T}\!\left<t\right>=t
and we are done
since
\Pi\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\emptyset\vdash t:\sigma
holds by hypothesis.

\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},x:\mathcal{M}
with
\mathcal{M}\neq\left[\,\right]
:
Since
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma\right)
, then
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma^{\prime}\right)

and
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\mathcal{M}\right)
, thus there exists
\Pi_{u}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\emptyset\vdash u:\mathcal{M}
for some
u\in\Lambda_{\oc}
. Consider
the following derivation:

(es)

(abs)

\Pi\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma^{\prime},x:\mathcal{M}\vdash t:\sigma
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\lambda x.t:\mathcal{M}\Rightarrow\sigma

\Pi_{u}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\emptyset\vdash u:\mathcal{M}
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash(\lambda x.t)\,u:\sigma

By the
i.h.
on
\Gamma^{\prime}
, one deduces that there exists
\Pi^{\prime}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\emptyset\vdash\mathtt{T}^{\prime}\!\left<(\lambda x.t)\,u\right>:\sigma
. This concludes
this case by taking
\mathtt{T}=\mathtt{T}^{\prime}\!\left<(\lambda x.\diamond)\,u\right>
.
∎

Lemma 48
.

Let
\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma\vdash t:\sigma
. If

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\sigma\right)\right)
, then there are a
testing context
\mathtt{T}
and a derivation
\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{T}\!\left<t\right>:\tau
for some type
\tau
not functional.

Proof.

Let
\Pi\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma\vdash t:\sigma
. We reason by induction
on
\sigma
:

\sigma=\alpha
or
\sigma=\mathcal{M}
: We set
\mathtt{T}\coloneqq\diamond
and
\tau\coloneqq\sigma
which concludes this
case since
\tau
is not functional,
\mathtt{T}\!\left<t\right>=t
and

\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma\vdash t:\sigma
holds by hypothesis.

\sigma=\mathcal{M}\Rightarrow\mu
: Since

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{B}}(\sigma)\right)
, then

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{B}}(\mu)\right)
and
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\mathcal{M}\right)
thus
there exists
\Pi_{u}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\emptyset\vdash u:\mathcal{M}
for some

u\in\Lambda_{\oc}
. Consider the following derivation:

(app)

\Pi\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma\vdash t:\mathcal{M}\Rightarrow\mu
\Pi_{u}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\emptyset\vdash u:\mathcal{M}
\Gamma\vdash t\,u:\mu

By
i.h.
on
\mu
, there is a testing context
\mathtt{T}^{\prime}
and a
derivation
\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{T}^{\prime}\!\left<t\,u\right>:\tau
for some type
\tau
not functional. Setting
\mathtt{T}=\mathtt{T}^{\prime}\!\left<\diamond\,u\right>
concludes this case.
∎

See
6

Proof.

We prove the stronger statement below, to have the right induction
hypothesis:

Let
\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{T}\!\left<t\right>:\sigma
with

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma\right)
and

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{B}}(\sigma)\right)
, then
\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t:\sigma^{\prime}
with
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma^{\prime}\right)
and

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{B}}(\sigma^{\prime})\right)
.

From that,

Lemma

6

follows immediately, because
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\emptyset\right)
is
vacuously true.

Let
\Pi\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{T}\!\left<t\right>:\sigma
with

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma\right)
and
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{B}}(\sigma)\right)
.
We reason by induction on
\mathtt{T}
:

\mathtt{T}=\diamond
: Trivial by hypothesis.

\mathtt{T}=(\lambda x.\mathtt{T}^{\prime})\,u
: Then

\Pi
has the following form:

(app)

(abs)

\Pi_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{1};x:\mathcal{M}\vdash\mathtt{T}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>:\sigma
\Gamma_{1}\vdash\lambda x.\mathtt{T}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>:\mathcal{M}\Rightarrow\sigma

\Pi_{2}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{2}\vdash u:\mathcal{M}
\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\vdash(\lambda x.\mathtt{T}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>)\,u:\sigma

with
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
. By hypothesis

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma\right)
, thus in particular

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma_{1}\right)
and
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma_{2}\right)
. Using

Lemma

47
on
\Pi_{2}
,
one deduces
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\mathcal{M}\right)
and thus
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma_{1};x:\mathcal{M}\right)
. By
i.h.
on
\Pi_{1}
, one obtains
\Pi^{\prime}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t:\sigma^{\prime}
with
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma^{\prime}\right)
and

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{B}}(\sigma^{\prime})\right)
.

\mathtt{T}=\mathtt{T}^{\prime}u
: Then
\Pi
has the
following form:

(app)

\Pi_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{1}\vdash\mathtt{T}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>:\mathcal{M}\Rightarrow\sigma
\Pi_{2}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{2}\vdash u:\mathcal{M}
\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\vdash\mathtt{T}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>u:\sigma

with
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
. By hypothesis

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma\right)
, thus in particular

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma_{1}\right)
and
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma_{2}\right)
. Using

Lemma

47
on
\Pi_{2}
,
one deduces
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\mathcal{M}\right)
, and thus

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{B}}(\mathcal{M}\Rightarrow\sigma)\right)
. By the
i.h.
on

\Pi_{1}
, one obtains
\Pi^{\prime}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t:\sigma^{\prime}

with
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma^{\prime}\right)
and

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{B}}(\sigma^{\prime})\right)
.
∎

Lemma 49
.

Let
\Pi\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\emptyset\vdash t:\sigma
where
\sigma

is not functional. If
t
is a
\mathtt{S}
-normal form,
then
t=\oc u
for some
u\in\Lambda_{\oc}
.

Proof.

We prove the stronger statement
below:

Let
\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\emptyset\vdash t:\sigma
with
\sigma
not
functional (resp. functional). If
t
is a

\mathtt{S}
-normal form, then
t=\oc u
(resp.
t=\lambda x.u
) for some
u\in\Lambda_{\oc}
.

Let
\Pi\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\emptyset\vdash t:\sigma
. By induction on

t
:

t=x
: Then
\Pi
can only have the following form:

(var)

x:\left[\sigma\right]\vdash x:\sigma

which contradicts the hypothesis that the type environment of

\Pi
is empty.

t=\lambda x.t^{\prime}
: Then
\Pi
has the following form:

(abs)

\Pi^{\prime}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}x:\mathcal{N}\vdash t^{\prime}:\tau
\emptyset\vdash\lambda x.t^{\prime}:\mathcal{N}\Rightarrow\tau

where
\sigma=\mathcal{N}\Rightarrow\tau
is functional, which concludes
this case.

t=t_{1}t_{2}
: Then in particular
t_{1}
is a

\mathtt{S}
-normal form and
\Pi
has the following form:

(app)

\Pi_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\emptyset\vdash t_{1}:\mathcal{N}\Rightarrow\sigma
\Pi_{2}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\emptyset\vdash t_{2}:\mathcal{N}
\emptyset\vdash t_{1}t_{2}:\sigma

By the
i.h.
on
t_{1}
,
t_{1}=\lambda x.t^{\prime}_{1}
which contradicts the
hypothesis that
t
is a
\mathtt{S}
-normal form.

t=t_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]
: Then in particular
t_{2}
is a

\mathtt{S}
-normal form and
\Pi
has the following form:

(es)

\Pi_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}x:\mathcal{M}\vdash t_{1}:\sigma
\Pi_{2}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\emptyset\vdash t_{2}:\mathcal{M}
\emptyset\vdash t_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]:\sigma

By the
i.h.
on
t_{2}
,
t_{2}=\oc t^{\prime}_{2}
which contradicts the
hypothesis that
t
is a
\mathtt{S}
-normal form.

t={\tt der}\!\left(t^{\prime}\right)
: Then
t^{\prime}
is a
\mathtt{S}
-normal
form and
\Pi
has the following form:

(der)

\Pi^{\prime}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\emptyset\vdash t^{\prime}:\left[\sigma\right]
\emptyset\vdash{\tt der}\!\left(t^{\prime}\right):\sigma

By the
i.h.
on
t^{\prime}
,
t^{\prime}=\oc t^{\prime}
which contradicts the hypothesis
that
t
is a
\mathtt{S}
-normal form.

t=\oc t^{\prime}
: Then
\Pi
has the following form:

(bg)

\left(\vrule height=7.5pt,depth=1.5pt,width=0.0pt\right.
\left.\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{}\hbox{$\Pi_{i}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\emptyset\vdash u:\tau_{i}$}}}$}\right)_{i\in I}
\quad\emptyset\vdash\oc t:\left[\tau_{i}\right]_{i\in I}\quad

where
\sigma=\left[\tau_{i}\right]_{i\in I}
is not functional, which
concludes this case.
∎

See
7

Proof.

(\Rightarrow)

Let
t
be meaningful. Then there exists a
testing context
\mathtt{T}
and a term
u\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such
that
\mathtt{T}\!\left<t\right>\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathrm{S}}\oc u
. Using rule
(bg) without any premises, the following typing
derivation holds
\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\emptyset\vdash\oc u:\left[\,\right]
. By subject expansion
(
Theorem

3
.
1
), one
deduces that
\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\emptyset\vdash\mathtt{T}\!\left<t\right>:\left[\,\right]
. Finally, by

Lemma

6
,
one concludes that
\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma\vdash t:\sigma
with

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma\right)
and

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\sigma\right)\right)
, so that

(\Gamma;\sigma)
is
\mathcal{B}
-testable.

(\Leftarrow)

Let
\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma\vdash t:\sigma
with
that
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma\right)
and

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\sigma\right)\right)
. By

Lemma

47
, one deduces
that
\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\emptyset\vdash\mathtt{T}\!\left<t\right>:\sigma
, and by

Lemma

48
,
\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\emptyset\vdash\mathtt{T}^{\prime}\!\left<\mathtt{T}\!\left<t\right>\right>:\tau
for some type

\tau
not functional. By

Theorem

3
.
2
, there
exists a
\mathtt{S}
-normal form
s\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such
that
\mathtt{T}^{\prime}\!\left<\mathtt{T}\!\left<t\right>\right>\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathrm{S}}s
. Moreover,
by
Theorem

3
.
1
,
one has that
\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\emptyset\vdash s:\tau
thus, using

Lemma

49
, one concludes
that
s=\oc u
for some
u\in\Lambda_{\oc}
. Since testing
contexts are stable by composition, we conclude that
t
is

dBang
-meaningful.
∎

Definition 50
.

For every
t\in\Lambda_{\oc}
, let
\llbracket t\rrbracket\coloneqq\{(\Gamma;\sigma)\ \mathcal{B}\text{-testable}\mid\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\;\Gamma\vdash t:\sigma\}
. The

equational theory
induced by system
\mathcal{B}
is
the relation
\equiv_{\mathcal{B}}
on
\Lambda_{\oc}
defined
by:
t\equiv_{\mathcal{B}}u
iff
\llbracket t\rrbracket=\llbracket u\rrbracket
.

Lemma 51
.

The equational theory
\equiv_{\mathcal{B}}
induced by

\mathcal{B}
is a consistent
\bm{\lambda}_{\texttt{dBang}}
-theory.

Proof.

By definition,
\equiv_{\mathcal{B}}
is an equivalence on

\Lambda_{\oc}
. It contains
\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}}
(that is, if
t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}}u
then
t\equiv_{\mathcal{B}}u
) by

Theorem

3
.(
1
).
From the definition of system
\mathcal{B}
, it follows
immediately that
t\equiv_{\mathcal{B}}u
implies

\mathtt{F}\!\left<t\right>\equiv_{\mathcal{B}}\mathtt{F}\!\left<u\right>
. We have
seen in
Section

3
that
\llbracket xx\rrbracket=\emptyset
and
\llbracket\lambda x.x\oc x\rrbracket\neq\emptyset
, hence
xx\not\equiv_{\mathcal{B}}\lambda x.x\oc x
and so
\equiv_{\mathcal{B}}
is consistent.
∎

See
8

Proof.

According to
Theorem

7
, for every
t\in\Lambda_{\oc}
,
\llbracket t\rrbracket=\emptyset
iff
t
is

dBang
-meaningless. Therefore,

\equiv_{\mathcal{B}}
equates all

dBang
-meaningless terms. Since
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}
is the
smallest
\bm{\lambda}_{\texttt{dBang}}
-theory equating all

dBang
-meaningless terms, and since

\equiv_{\mathcal{B}}
is a
\bm{\lambda}_{\texttt{dBang}}
-theory
(
Lemma

51
), then
\equiv_{\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}}\,\subseteq\,\equiv_{\mathcal{B}}
. Hence, since

\equiv_{\mathcal{B}}
is consistent
(
Lemma

51
), then so is
\equiv_{\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}}
.
∎

Appendix C
Proofs of
Section

4

See
9

Proof.

(\Leftarrow)

Immediate consequence of the definition of

\Pi\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}_{\tt m}}\Gamma\vdash t:\sigma
.

(\Rightarrow)

Let
\Pi\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma\vdash t:\sigma
with
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma\right)
and

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\sigma\right)\right)
. We proceed by induction
on
\Pi
, and we consider its last rule. Cases:

(var)

Then
t=x
and
\Pi
has the
following form:

(var)

x:\left[\sigma\right]\vdash x:\sigma

with
\Gamma=x:\left[\sigma\right]
. By hypothesis

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(x:\left[\sigma\right]\right)
and

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\sigma\right)\right)
, hence
x:\left[\sigma\right]\vdash x:\sigma
, which is the only judgment
of
\Pi
, is
\mathcal{B}
-testable. Therefore,
\Pi\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}_{\tt m}}\Gamma\vdash t:\sigma
.

(abs)

Then
t=\lambda x.t^{\prime}
and
\Pi
has
the following form, with
\sigma=\mathcal{M}\Rightarrow\tau
:

(abs)

\Phi\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma,x:\mathcal{M}\vdash t^{\prime}:\tau
\Gamma\vdash\lambda x.t^{\prime}:\mathcal{M}\Rightarrow\tau

By hypothesis
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma\right)
and

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\mathcal{M}\Rightarrow\tau\right)\right)
, thus

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\mathcal{M}\right)
and

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\tau\right)\right)
, hence

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma,x:\mathcal{M}\right)
. By
i.h.

\Phi\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}_{\tt m}}\Gamma,x:\mathcal{M}\vdash t^{\prime}:\tau
. So, all
judgments in
\Pi
are
\mathcal{B}
-testable, hence
\Pi\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}_{\tt m}}\Gamma\vdash t:\sigma
.

(app)

Then
t=t_{1}t_{2}
and
\Pi

has the following form, with
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
:

(app)

\Phi_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{1}\vdash t_{1}:\mathcal{M}\Rightarrow\sigma
\Phi_{2}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{2}\vdash t_{2}:\mathcal{M}
\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\vdash t_{1}t_{2}:\sigma

By hypothesis
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\right)
and

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\sigma\right)\right)
thus

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma_{1}\right)
and
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma_{2}\right)
.
By
Lemma

47

applied to
\Phi_{2}
,
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\mathcal{M}\right)
and hence

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\mathcal{M}\Rightarrow\sigma\right)\right)
. By

i.h.
on both
\Phi_{1}
and
\Phi_{2}
,
\Phi_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}_{\tt m}}\Gamma_{1}\vdash t_{1}:\mathcal{M}\Rightarrow\sigma
and
\Phi_{2}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}_{\tt m}}\Gamma_{2}\vdash t_{2}:\mathcal{M}
. So, all judgments in

\Pi
are
\mathcal{B}
-testable, hence
\Pi\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}_{\tt m}}\Gamma\vdash t:\sigma
.

(es)

Then
t=t_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]
and
\Pi

has the following form, with
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
:

(es)

\Phi_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{1};x:\mathcal{M}\vdash t_{1}:\sigma
\Phi_{2}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma_{2}\vdash t_{2}:\mathcal{M}
\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\vdash t_{1}[x\backslash t_{2}]:\sigma

By hypothesis
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\right)
and

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\sigma\right)\right)
thus

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma_{1}\right)
and
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma_{2}\right)
.
By
Lemma

47

applied to
\Phi_{2}
,
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\mathcal{M}\right)
and hence

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{B}}\!\left(\Gamma_{1},x:\mathcal{M}\right)
. By
i.h.
on both
\Phi_{1}

and
\Phi_{2}
,
\Phi_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}_{\tt m}}\Gamma_{1},x:\mathcal{M}\vdash t_{1}:\sigma
and
\Phi_{2}\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}_{\tt m}}\Gamma_{2}\vdash t_{2}:\mathcal{M}
. So, all judgments in
\Pi
are

\mathcal{B}

(der)

Lemma 52
.

Let
t,u\in\Lambda_{\oc}
. If the smallest
\bm{\lambda}_{\texttt{dBang}}
-theory
containing
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}
and equating
t
and
u
is consistent,
then
t\equiv_{\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}^{*}}u
.

Proof.

Let
t,u\in\Lambda_{\oc}
and
\mathcal{E}
be the smallest

\bm{\lambda}_{\texttt{dBang}}
-theory containing
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}
and equating
t
and

u
. Suppose by contrapositive that
t\not\equiv_{\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}^{*}}u
, that is, there is a context
\mathtt{F}
such that

\mathtt{F}\!\left<t\right>
is
dBang
-meaningful and

\mathtt{F}\!\left<u\right>
is
dBang
-meaningless. We show that

\mathcal{E}
is inconsistent.

Let
s\in\Lambda_{\oc}
be a
dBang
-meaningful term
(as for example
\oc z
) and
x\in\mathcal{X}
fresh. On
one hand, by definition of meaningfulness, there exists a testing
context
\mathtt{T}
such that
\mathtt{T}\!\left<\mathtt{F}\!\left<t\right>\right>\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathrm{S}}\oc u
for some term
u\in\Lambda_{\oc}
. In
particular,
(\lambda x.s)\,\mathtt{T}\!\left<\mathtt{F}\!\left<t\right>\right>\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathrm{S}}(\lambda x.s)\,\oc u\rightarrow^{*}_{\mathrm{S}}s
. Since

\mathcal{E}
is a
\bm{\lambda}_{\texttt{dBang}}
-theory, then

(\lambda x.s)\,\mathtt{T}\!\left<\mathtt{F}\!\left<t\right>\right>\equiv_{\mathcal{E}}s
. Since
\mathtt{F}\!\left<u\right>
is

dBang
-meaningless, then so are

\mathtt{T}\!\left<\mathtt{F}\!\left<u\right>\right>
. By
typing, we deduce that

(\lambda x.s)\,\mathtt{T}\!\left<\mathtt{F}\!\left<u\right>\right>
is also

dBang
-meaningless. Since
t\equiv_{\mathcal{E}}u

then by contextuality and transitivity

(\lambda x.s)\,\mathtt{T}\!\left<\mathtt{F}\!\left<u\right>\right>\equiv_{\mathcal{E}}s
. Since
\mathcal{E}
equates all
meaningless terms, one concludes by transitivity that
\Omega\equiv_{\mathcal{E}}s
. Since
s
is an arbitrary meaningful
term,
\mathcal{E}
is inconsistent.
∎

Corollary 53
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a consistent
\bm{\lambda}_{\texttt{dBang}}
-theory. If

\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}\subseteq\mathcal{E}
then
\mathcal{E}\subseteq\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}^{*}
.

Proof.

Let
t,u\in\Lambda_{\oc}
such that
t\equiv_{\mathcal{E}}u
.
Let us show that
t\equiv_{\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}^{*}}u
(hence
\mathcal{E}\subseteq\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}^{*}
). Let
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}^{t,u}
be the
smallest
\bm{\lambda}_{\texttt{dBang}}
-theory containing
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}
and equating

t
and
u
. Note that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}^{t,u}\subseteq\mathcal{E}
.
Hence, since by hypothesis
\mathcal{E}
does not equate all
terms,
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}^{t,u}
is consistent too. Therefore,
t\equiv_{\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}^{*}}u
by

Lemma

52
.
∎

See
12

Proof.

(
\bm{\lambda}_{\texttt{dBang}}
-theory):

•

From its definition, it follows immediately that

\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}^{*}
is an equivalence on
\Lambda_{\oc}
.

•

To prove that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}^{*}
is closed under full
contexts, suppose that
t\equiv_{\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}^{*}}u
: we
have to prove that, for every full context
\mathtt{F}
,

\mathtt{F}\!\left<t\right>\equiv_{\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}^{*}}\mathtt{F}\!\left<u\right>
; that
is, for every full contexts
\mathtt{F},\mathtt{F}^{\prime}
,

\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<\mathtt{F}\!\left<t\right>\right>
is

dBang
-meaningful iff so is

\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<\mathtt{F}\!\left<u\right>\right>
. Since

\mathtt{F}^{\prime}\!\left<\mathtt{F}\right>
is a full context and
t\equiv_{\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}^{*}}u
, we are done by definition.

•

To prove that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}^{*}
contains
\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}}
,
suppose that
t\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}}u
, so that
\mathtt{F}\!\left<t\right>\rightarrow_{\mathrm{F}}\mathtt{F}\!\left<u\right>
holds for every context

\mathtt{F}
. By

Theorem

3
.(
1
),
for every typing (and in particular every testable typing)

(\Gamma;\sigma)
,
\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{F}\!\left<t\right>:\sigma
iff
\triangleright_{\mathcal{B}}\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{F}\!\left<u\right>:\sigma
. Hence, by

Theorem

7
,
\mathtt{F}\!\left<t\right>
is

dBang
-meaningful iff so is
\mathtt{F}\!\left<u\right>
;
hence,
t\equiv_{\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}^{*}}u
by definition.

(Containing
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}
):

As
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}^{*}
is also a

\bm{\lambda}_{\texttt{dBang}}
-theory, it suffices to prove that
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}^{*}

equates all
dBang
-meaningless terms. Let
t,u\in\Lambda_{\oc}
be
dBang
-meaningless. By surface
genericity (
Corollary

11
),
for every full context
\mathtt{F}
,
\mathtt{F}\!\left<t\right>
is

dBang
-meaningful iff so is
\mathtt{F}\!\left<u\right>
;
therefore,
t\equiv_{\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}^{*}}u
by definition.

(Consistency):

The term
\oc x
is trivially

dBang
-meaningful, while we have seen in

Section

3
that
\Omega
is

dBang
-meaningless. Take
\mathtt{F}=\diamond
, then

\mathtt{F}\!\left<\oc x\right>=\oc x
is
dBang
-meaningful
and
\mathtt{F}\!\left<\Omega\right>=\Omega
is

dBang
-meaningless. Therefore,
\oc x\not\equiv_{\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}^{*}}\Omega
.

(Maximality):

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a consistent

\bm{\lambda}_{\texttt{dBang}}
-theory containing
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}
. By

Corollary

53
,
\mathcal{E}\subseteq\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}^{*}
. Therefore,
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}^{*}
is maximal among
the consistent
\bm{\lambda}_{\texttt{dBang}}
-theories containing
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}
.

(Uniqueness):

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a
maximal

consistent
\bm{\lambda}_{\texttt{dBang}}
-theory containing
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}
. By

Corollary

53
,
\mathcal{E}\subseteq\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}^{*}
. We conclude that
\mathcal{E}=\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}^{*}

by maximality of
\mathcal{E}
. Therefore,
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}^{*}
is
unique among the maximal consistent
\bm{\lambda}_{\texttt{dBang}}
-theories
containing
\bm{\mathcal{H}}_{\texttt{dBang}}
.
∎

Lemma 54
.

Let
(\Pi_{i}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{i}\vdash t:\sigma_{i})_{i\in I}
with
I
finite.
If
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i}\right)
then

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(\left[\sigma_{i}\right]_{i\in I}\right)
.

Proof.

Let
(\Pi_{i}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{i}\vdash t:\sigma_{i})_{i\in I}
such
that
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i}\right)
. We reason by induction
on the number of variables in
{\tt dom}(+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i})
:

If
{\tt dom}(+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i})
is empty, then
\Gamma_{i}=\emptyset
for all
i\in I
. Thus
\Pi_{i}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\emptyset\vdash t:\sigma_{i}
for all
i\in I
by hypothesis, hence
\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\emptyset\vdash\oc t:[\sigma_{i}]_{i\in I}
by applying the rule
(bg)
. Therefore,
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(\left[\sigma_{i}\right]_{i\in I}\right)
.

If
{\tt dom}(+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i})
is not empty,
then every
\Gamma_{i}=\Gamma^{\prime}_{i},x:\mathcal{M}_{i}
(with at least one

\mathcal{M}_{j}\neq\left[\,\right]
): Since
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i\in I}\right)
, then
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(+_{i\in I}\Gamma^{\prime}_{i\in I}\right)
and
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(\biguplus_{i\in I}\mathcal{M}_{i}\right)
. Let
\mathcal{M}_{i}=\left[\rho_{i}^{j}\right]_{j\in J_{i}}
, then by definition, there exists

(\Pi_{i}^{j}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\emptyset\vdash u:\rho_{i}^{j})_{i\in I,j\in J_{i}}
. Let
\Phi_{i}
be the following derivation:

(es)

(abs)

\Pi_{i}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{i},x:\left[\rho_{i}^{j}\right]_{j\in J_{i}}\vdash t:\sigma_{i}
\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}\vdash\lambda x.t:\left[\rho_{i}^{j}\right]_{j\in J_{i}}\Rightarrow\sigma_{i}

(\Pi_{i}^{j}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\emptyset\vdash u:\rho_{i}^{j})_{j\in J_{i}}
\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}\vdash(\lambda x.t)\,u:\sigma_{i}

We thus obtain
(\Phi_{i}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}\vdash(\lambda x.t)\,u:\sigma_{i})_{i\in I}
and by
i.h.
on the
number of variables in
{\tt dom}(+_{i\in I}\Gamma^{\prime}_{i})
, one concludes that

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(\left[\sigma_{i}\right]_{i\in I}\right)
.
∎

Lemma 55
.

Let
t\in\Lambda
and
\mathtt{T}
be a testing context. If

\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\;\emptyset\vdash\mathtt{T}\!\left<t\right>:\sigma
with

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(\sigma\right)\right)
, then there exist
\Gamma

and
\sigma^{\prime}
such that
\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\;\Gamma\vdash t:\sigma^{\prime}

with
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(\Gamma\right)
and

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(\sigma^{\prime}\right)\right)
.

Proof.

We prove the stronger statement below, to have the right induction
hypothesis:

Let
\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{T}\!\left<t\right>:\sigma
with

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(\Gamma\right)
and

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(\sigma\right)\right)
,
then there exists
\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\sigma^{\prime}
such that
\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t:\sigma^{\prime}
with
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(\Gamma^{\prime}\right)
and

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(\sigma^{\prime}\right)\right)
.

From that,

Lemma

55

follows immediately, because
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(\emptyset\right)
is vacuously
true.

Let
\Pi\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{T}\!\left<t\right>:\sigma
with

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(\Gamma\right)
and
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(\sigma\right)\right)
.
We reason by induction on
\mathtt{T}
:

\mathtt{T}=\diamond
: Trivial by hypothesis by taking

\Gamma^{\prime}:=\Gamma
and
\sigma^{\prime}:=\sigma
.

\mathtt{T}=(\lambda x.\mathtt{T}^{\prime})\,u
: Then

\Pi
has the following form, where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{2}^{i}
and
I
is finite:

(app)

(abs)

\Pi_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{1},x:\left[\tau_{i}\right]_{i\in I}\vdash\mathtt{T}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>:\sigma
\Gamma_{1}\vdash\lambda x.\mathtt{T}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>:\left[\tau_{i}\right]_{i\in i}\Rightarrow\sigma

(\Pi_{2}^{i}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{2}^{i}\vdash u:\tau_{i})_{i\in I}
\Gamma_{1}+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{2}^{i}\vdash(\lambda x.\mathtt{T}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>)\,u:\sigma

By hypothesis

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(\Gamma\right)
, thus in particular

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(\Gamma_{1}\right)
and
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{2}^{i}\right)
. Using

Lemma

54
on

(\Pi_{2}^{i})_{i\in I}
, one deduces that

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(\left[\tau_{i}\right]_{i\in I}\right)
thus

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(\Gamma_{1},x:\left[\tau_{i}\right]_{i\in I}\right)
. By
i.h.
on
\Pi_{1}
, one obtains
\Pi^{\prime}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t:\sigma^{\prime}
with
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(\Gamma^{\prime}\right)
and

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left({\tt args}\left(\sigma^{\prime}\right)\right)
.

\mathtt{T}=\mathtt{T}^{\prime}u
: Then
\Pi
has the
following form, with
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{2}^{i}
:

(app)

\Pi_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{1}\vdash\mathtt{T}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>:\left[\tau_{i}\right]_{i\in I}\Rightarrow\sigma
(\Pi_{2}^{i}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma_{2}^{i}\vdash u:\tau_{i})_{i\in I}
\Gamma_{1}+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{2}^{i}\vdash\mathtt{T}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>u:\sigma

By hypothesis

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(\Gamma\right)
, thus in particular

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(\Gamma_{1}\right)
and
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{2}^{i}\right)
. By

Lemma

54
on

(\Pi_{2}^{i})_{i\in I}
,

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(\left[\tau_{i}\right]_{i\in I}\right)
, and since

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(\sigma\right)\right)
thus

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(\left[\tau_{i}\right]_{i\in I}\Rightarrow\sigma\right)\right)
. By the
i.h.
on
\Pi_{1}
, one obtains
\Pi^{\prime}\triangleright_{\mathcal{N}}\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t:\sigma^{\prime}
with
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left(\Gamma^{\prime}\right)
and

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{N}}\!\left({\tt args}\left(\sigma^{\prime}\right)\right)
.
∎

Section

Lemma 56
.

Let
\Pi\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma\vdash t:\sigma
. If

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\Gamma\right)
then
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\sigma\right)
.

Proof.

Let
\Pi\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma\vdash t:\sigma
. We reason by induction
on the number of variables in
{\tt dom}(\Gamma)
:

\Gamma=\emptyset
: Then
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\sigma\right)

holds by hypothesis.

\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},x:\mathcal{M}
with
\mathcal{M}\neq\left[\,\right]
:
Since
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\Gamma\right)
, then
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\Gamma^{\prime}\right)

and
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\mathcal{M}\right)
, thus there exists
\Pi_{u}\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\emptyset\vdash u:\mathcal{M}
for some
u\in\Lambda
. Consider
the following derivation:

(es)

(abs)

\Pi\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma^{\prime},x:\mathcal{M}\vdash t:\sigma
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\lambda x.t:\left[\mathcal{M}\Rightarrow\sigma\right]

\Pi_{u}\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\emptyset\vdash u:\mathcal{M}
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash(\lambda x.t)\,u:\sigma

By the
i.h.
on
\Gamma^{\prime}
, one concludes that

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\sigma\right)
.
∎

Lemma 57
.

Let
t\in\Lambda
and
\mathtt{T}
be a testing context.
If
\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\emptyset\vdash\mathtt{T}\!\left<t\right>:\sigma
with

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\sigma\right)
, then there exist
\Gamma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
such that
\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma\vdash t:\sigma^{\prime}
with
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\Gamma\right)
and

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\sigma^{\prime}\right)\right)
.

Proof.

We prove the stronger statement below, to have the right induction
hypothesis:

Let
\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{T}\!\left<t\right>:\sigma
with

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\Gamma\right)
and

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\sigma\right)\right)
, then there exist
\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\sigma^{\prime}
such that
\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t:\sigma^{\prime}
with
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\Gamma^{\prime}\right)
and

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\sigma^{\prime}\right)\right)
.

From that,

Lemma

57

follows immediately, because
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\emptyset\right)
is vacuously
true.

Let
\Pi\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma\vdash\mathtt{T}\!\left<t\right>:\sigma
with

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\Gamma\right)
and
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\sigma\right)\right)
. We
reason by induction on
\mathtt{T}
:

\mathtt{T}=\diamond
: Trivial by hypothesis.

\mathtt{T}=(\lambda x.\mathtt{T}^{\prime})\,u
: Then

\Pi
has the following form:

(app)

(abs)

\Pi_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma_{1},x:\mathcal{M}\vdash\mathtt{T}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>:\sigma
\Gamma_{1}\vdash\lambda x.\mathtt{T}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>:\left[\mathcal{M}\Rightarrow\sigma\right]

\Pi_{2}\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma_{2}\vdash u:\mathcal{M}
\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\vdash(\lambda x.\mathtt{T}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>)\,u:\sigma

with
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
. By hypothesis

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\Gamma\right)
, thus in particular

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\Gamma_{1}\right)
and
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\Gamma_{2}\right)
. Using

Lemma

56
on
\Pi_{2}
,
one deduces
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\mathcal{M}\right)
and thus

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\Gamma_{1},x:\mathcal{M}\right)
. By
i.h.
on
\Pi_{1}
, one
obtains
\Pi^{\prime}\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t:\sigma^{\prime}
with

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\Gamma^{\prime}\right)
and

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\sigma^{\prime}\right)\right)
.

\mathtt{T}=\mathtt{T}^{\prime}u
: Then
\Pi
has the
following form:

(app)

\Pi_{1}\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma_{1}\vdash\mathtt{T}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>:\left[\mathcal{M}\Rightarrow\sigma\right]
\Pi_{2}\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma_{2}\vdash u:\mathcal{M}
\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\vdash\mathtt{T}^{\prime}\!\left<t\right>u:\sigma

with
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
. By hypothesis

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\Gamma\right)
, thus in particular

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\Gamma_{1}\right)
and
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\Gamma_{2}\right)
.
By
Lemma

56
,
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\mathcal{M}\right)
.
As
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\sigma\right)\right)
by hypothesis and
{\tt args}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\mathcal{M}\Rightarrow\sigma\right)=\{\mathcal{M}\}\cup{\tt args}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\sigma\right)
, then
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\mathcal{M}\Rightarrow\sigma\right)\right)

and
thus, by the
i.h.
on
\Pi_{1}
, one obtains
\Pi^{\prime}\triangleright_{\mathcal{V}}\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t:\sigma^{\prime}
with
{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\Gamma^{\prime}\right)
and

{\tt inh}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left({\tt args}_{\mathcal{V}}\!\left(\sigma^{\prime}\right)\right)
.
∎
